[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1,80p' .skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.1.0.0.md
---
title: "v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover"
description: "Claude Code hands over by default, vendored installs get stricter overlay checks and search says what it left out. Skilled also needs a patched Node.js and keeps .env values away from agents on all eight runtimes."
trigger_phrases:
  - "v4.1.0.0 release notes"
  - "skilled v4.1.0.0"
  - "dotenv secret guard"
  - "patched node releases required"
  - "goal carried across a hop"
  - "strict overlay writes"
  - "hub skill names its command"
  - "lookup reports total candidates"
  - "mistral vibe runtime hooks"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"

# v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover

Long sessions now hand over cleanly, and Claude Code does it by default.

- Near its context limit, a session writes a handoff note and a fresh one picks up in the same terminal.
- The hop carries the running command, the goal and the session's name.
- Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe now finish a hop too.

The skill advisor and the deep loops now say more and guess less.

- The advisor names the slash command behind a hub skill.
- It says whether the agent may run that command, start it or should ask first.
- Mistral Vibe joins as the eighth runtime, with Skilled's guards and advisor brief in every session.
- A deep review fails while a required check still fails, and a script makes that call.

Search, the spec commands and the overlay checks now report what really happened.

- Search answers say how much they left out.
- The spec commands save what they report and check their own work.
- Overlay checks in a vendored project refuse a bad write before it lands.

This release also changes what you install and what an agent may read.

- Skilled now needs a patched Node.js release.
- A new guard keeps the values in a `.env` file away from every agent, on all eight runtimes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> Six changes can break an existing setup. Read these before you update.
>
> 1. Old session relaunch switch names are no longer read. [Old switch names](#c-old-switch-names-stop-working)
> 2. An open handoff note in the old state folder is not found. [Moving from session relaunch](#c-moving-from-session-relaunch)
> 3. The history lookup is no longer offered after a compaction. [Claimed note history](#c-a-claimed-note-offers-the-senders-history)
> 4. `/deep:ai-council` is gone, so run `/speckit:ai-council`. [AI Council](#c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent)
> 5. The standalone git pre-commit hook is gone. [Hook folders](#c-folders-say-what-they-guard)
> 6. Code Mode needs Node 24.22.1 or newer on 24. [Node.js](#c-patched-releases-are-required)

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

Several parts of Skilled said one thing and did another. Handover existed, but most people never had it on. Checks could pass while something underneath had failed. This release closes those gaps.

- Handover shipped off, so most Claude Code users never had it.
- A hop mid-command came back as a plain chat.
- The advisor named a skill, not the command to type.
- A deep review could pass while a required check failed.
- A search cut at its limit never said what it hid.
- An overlay re-pin could write a wrong value and still pass.
- Any agent could read `.env` and send its secrets to the model.
- Skilled still accepted Node 20 after an October security patch.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Claude Code hands over by default](#c-claude-code-hands-over-by-default) | A hop keeps the running command, the goal and the session's name. | Session Handover |
| [Old switch names are no longer read](#c-old-switch-names-stop-working) | Rename each switch and move any open handoff note. | Session Handover |
| [Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe now finish a hop](#c-pi-opencode-and-vibe-finish-a-hop) | Each completes the same hop as Claude Code, with no input from you. | Session Handover |
| [A hub skill names the command to type](#c-a-hub-skill-names-the-command-to-type) | The brief also says whether to run it, start it or ask first. | Skill Advisor |
| [Doctor checks show whether the brief arrives](#c-doctor-checks-show-whether-the-brief-arrives) | The setup doctor gains an embedder leg and a delivery leg. | Skill Advisor |
| [Mistral Vibe is the eighth runtime](#c-vibe-joins-as-a-runtime) | Its sessions run Skilled's guards, advisor brief and goal through hooks. | Mistral Vibe |
| [The AI Council is its own agent](#c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent) | Run `/speckit:ai-council` instead of `/deep:ai-council`. | Deep Loop |

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '240,270p' README.md && echo "=== 280-300 ===" && sed -n '284,300p' README.md
The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

### Create And Update Skills, Agents And Commands

Extending Skilled follows the same framework that built it. The [sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md) hub holds a template, a set of standards and a validator for each kind of component. Ask for a new skill, agent or command in plain words and the advisor loads the matching sk-doc mode, so the result matches the rest of the repository.

=== 280-300 ===

**[sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md)** authors documentation and components. Its fifteen modes create skills, parent hubs, READMEs, agents, commands, feature catalogs, manual testing playbooks, benchmarks, changelogs, diffs, frontmatter, repo rules and goals. Two more run quality control and apply the human voice rules.

**[sk-design](.skilled/skills/sk-design/SKILL.md)** turns one design identity into an artifact. Its three modes extract a measured style reference from a live site, draw a diagram and build a chart.

**[system-deep-loop](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md)** runs the autonomous loops over one shared runtime. Its two modes are deep research and deep review.

**[cli-external-orchestration](.skilled/skills/cli-external-orchestration/README.md)** hands work to another AI CLI. Its eight modes are bridges to OpenCode, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Devin, Pi, DeepSeek Harness and Mistral Vibe.

**[cli-classifier](.skilled/skills/cli-classifier/README.md)** asks a hosted classifier for typed judgments. It has one mode today, the Jev transport, and future classifiers join as new modes.

**[mcp-tooling](.skilled/skills/mcp-tooling/README.md)** bridges outside tools through Code Mode. Its eight modes cover Chrome DevTools, ClickUp, the Aside browser, Notion and Obsidian. Three more are read-only design sources: Figma, Refero and Mobbin.

### Standalone Skills

**[system-spec-kit](.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/README.md)** owns spec folders, documentation levels, the validator, continuity and lexical search. Every file change goes through it.

 The weighted score has to reach `compositeStopScore`, which defaults to 0.60, before the loop may consider a stop. Clearing the threshold is not enough on its own. +Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.
+
+`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1,240p' .skilled/commands/assets/local-skilled-alignment/check-release-coverage.cjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// MODULE: Check Release Coverage
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
'use strict';

// Judges a scope against the two root documents an operator actually reads: the
// open root release notes and the root README. A skill the branch adds, or a
// version bump it ships, shows up in the diff, but a reader learns about it only
// when those documents name it. A check that reads the touched lines alone
// cannot see that gap, so this one reads the documents whole.
//
// The open release note is the highest version under the root changelog folder,
// compared as dotted numbers rather than as text. Four-part versions ship in
// order, so v4.0.0.10 is newer than v4.0.0.9 and a lexical sort inverts the two.
//
// An id is matched as a whole word: a hit inside a longer id or a path fragment
// does not announce it, and the match is case-insensitive because a document may
// lead a sentence with the name.
//
// Usage: check-release-coverage.cjs --scope FILE [--repo DIR]
// --repo defaults to the current directory.
// Exit 0 every required id is described, 1 a required id is missing, 2 usage or
// input error, including no root release notes to read.

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const { fail, readJson } = require('./alignment-lib.cjs');

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const TAG = 'check-release-coverage';
const USAGE = 'Usage: check-release-coverage.cjs --scope FILE [--repo DIR]';
const NOTES_DIR = '.skilled/changelog/skilled';
const NOTES_NAME = /^v([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)*)\.md$/;
const SKILL_CHANGELOG = /^\.skilled\/skills\/(?:.+\/)?([^/]+)\/changelog\/v[0-9.]+\.md$/;
const SKILL_DOC = /^\.skilled\/skills\/(?:.+\/)?([^/]+)\/SKILL\.md$/;
const NAME_LINE = /^name:[ \t]*(.+?)[ \t]*$/m;

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. ARGUMENTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function parseArgs(argv) {
  const opts = { scope: null, repo: process.cwd() };
  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {
    const [flag, inline] = argv[i].split(/=(.*)/s, 2);
    const value = () => {
      const v = inline !== undefined ? inline : argv[++i];
      if (!v) fail(TAG, `${flag} needs a value\n${USAGE}`);
      return v;
    };
    if (flag === '--scope') opts.scope = value();
    else if (flag === '--repo') opts.repo = value();
    else fail(TAG, `unknown argument: ${argv[i]}\n${USAGE}`);
  }
  if (!opts.scope) fail(TAG, `--scope is required\n${USAGE}`);
  return opts;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. THE OPEN RELEASE NOTES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function versionParts(name) {
  return NOTES_NAME.exec(name)[1].split('.').map(Number);
}

// A shorter version pads with zeroes, so v4.0.0 and v4.0.0.0 rank equal.
function compareParts(left, right) {
  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(left.length, right.length); i += 1) {
    const diff = (left[i] || 0) - (right[i] || 0);
    if (diff !== 0) return diff;
  }
  return 0;
}

function newestName(names) {
  let newest = null;
  for (const name of names) {
    if (newest === null || compareParts(versionParts(name), versionParts(newest)) > 0) newest = name;
  }
  return newest;
}

/**
 * Read the open root release note. A folder with no versioned note is a checker
 * error: reporting every id as missing would blame the change for a document
 * that is not there.
 *
 * @param {string} repo - Resolved repository root
 * @returns {{file: string, text: string}} Absolute path and contents
 */
function openNotes(repo) {
  const dir = path.join(repo, NOTES_DIR);
  const names = fs.existsSync(dir) ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((name) => NOTES_NAME.test(name)) : [];
  if (names.length === 0) fail(TAG, `no root release notes under ${NOTES_DIR}`);
  const file = path.join(dir, newestName(names));
  return { file, text: fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8') };
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. REQUIREMENTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function escapeRegExp(text) {
  return text.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
}

// A whole-id hit: neither neighbour may extend it into a longer id or a path
// fragment, and the start and end of the document bound it on their side.
function appearsIn(text, id) {
  return new RegExp(`(?<![A-Za-z0-9_-])${escapeRegExp(id)}(?![A-Za-z0-9_-])`, 'i').test(text);
}

// The frontmatter name of the added SKILL.md, or null when it names none or the
// file is not on disk. The folder name is the fallback, so a document that is
// not readable yet still has to be announced under its folder.
function frontmatterName(repo, rel) {
  let text;
  try {
    text = fs.readFileSync(path.join(repo, rel), 'utf8');
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
  const block = text.match(/^---\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n---(?:\r?\n|$)/);
  if (!block) return null;
  const line = block[1].match(NAME_LINE);
  if (!line) return null;
  const name = line[1].replace(/^(['"])(.*)\1$/, '$2').trim();
  return name || null;
}

/**
 * Walk the scope's added paths and record which ids each root document must
 * name. An id is kept once per document even when several paths require it, and
 * the first path that required it travels with it so the gap names a real file.
 *
 * @param {Object} scope - Parsed scope output
 * @param {string} repo - Resolved repository root
 * @returns {{notes: Map<string, string>, readme: Map<string, string>}}
 */
function collectRequirements(scope, repo) {
  const statuses = scope && typeof scope === 'object' ? scope.statuses : null;
  if (!statuses || typeof statuses !== 'object' || Array.isArray(statuses)) {
    throw new Error('scope.json has no statuses map');
  }
  const notes = new Map();
  const readme = new Map();
  for (const [file, status] of Object.entries(statuses)) {
    if (status !== 'A') continue;
    const changelog = SKILL_CHANGELOG.exec(file);
    if (changelog) {
      if (!notes.has(changelog[1])) notes.set(changelog[1], file);
      continue;
    }
    const doc = SKILL_DOC.exec(file);
    if (!doc) continue;
    const id = frontmatterName(repo, file) || doc[1];
    if (!notes.has(id)) notes.set(id, file);
    if (!readme.has(id)) readme.set(id, file);
  }
  return { notes, readme };
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. MAIN
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function main() {
  const opts = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
  const repo = path.resolve(opts.repo);
  const scope = readJson(path.resolve(opts.scope));
  const notes = openNotes(repo);
  const required = collectRequirements(scope, repo);
  let readme = '';
  try {
    readme = fs.readFileSync(path.join(repo, 'README.md'), 'utf8');
  } catch {
    readme = '';
  }

  let missing = 0;
  for (const [id, file] of required.notes) {
    if (appearsIn(notes.text, id)) continue;
    console.log(`MISSING release-notes ${id} required by ${file}`);
    missing += 1;
  }
  for (const [id, file] of required.readme) {
    if (appearsIn(readme, id)) continue;
    console.log(`MISSING readme ${id} required by ${file}`);
    missing += 1;
  }

  const total = required.notes.size + required.readme.size;
  const notesRel = path.relative(repo, notes.file).split(path.sep).join('/');
  console.log(`STATUS=${missing > 0 ? 'FINDINGS' : 'OK'} ${TAG}: required=${total} missing=${missing} notes=${notesRel}`);
  return missing > 0 ? 1 : 0;
}

try {
  process.exitCode = main();
} catch (error) {
  fail(TAG, error && error.message ? error.message : String(error));
}

Seed 4 + 12 admissions in iterations 1-3 filled all 16 slots, each iteration answered one node, and the three init questions waited until iterations 15-16 because a frontier node outranks strategy focus. A non-cap stop is unreachable while the node cap equals the iteration cap.
- Fixes confirmed live: iteration 1 got a frontier block (fix c); `step_frontier_graph_upsert` exited 0 on all 16 iterations (fix a); all 16 nodes ended answered (fix b).
- Possible residue of fix b: F-2-015 and F-2-016 showed `exhausted/budget` after reduce 14 while already counted answered, and flipped to answered at reduce 15. Not confirmed.
- T032 on this run: `COMMAND_ROWS|TRACKED_COMMANDS` 0 in research.md (iteration-016.md:36 cites COMMAND_ROWS). The check depends on what the leaf cites; run 1 printed 1.
- Outside this phase: synthesis-closeout exited 2 (`count_only_state_findings_not_reconstructed`, needs `registry.metrics.sourceFindings`, which no single-executor producer writes); iteration 5's graph upsert hit a SQLite FOREIGN KEY error on edges to undeclared nodes (`coverage-graph-db.ts:760`).

} catch {
    return nodes;
  }

  for (const line of content.split('\n')) {
    const trimmed = line.trim();
    if (!trimmed) continue;
    let row;
    try {
      row = JSON.parse(trimmed);
    } catch {
      // A torn or hand-edited line must not hide the rows after it.
      continue;
    }
    if (!isPlainObject(row) || typeof row.nodeId !== 'string' || row.nodeId === '') continue;
    nodes.set(row.nodeId, row);
  }

  return nodes;
}

function readTargetConfig(artifactDir, target) {
  const configFile = target === 'review' ? REVIEW_CONFIG_FILE : CONFIG_FILE;
  try {
    return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(artifactDir, configFile), 'utf8'));
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

function readCaps(artifactDir, target = 'research') {
  const config = readTargetConfig(artifactDir, assertTarget(target));
  const source = isPlainObject(config) ? config : {};
  const expansive = isPlainObject(source.expansiveDepth) ? source.expansiveDepth : {};
  // Each node costs at least one iteration, so a cap equal to the iteration budget
  // fills every slot and leaves none for the seeded questions or the stop gates.
  const iterationBudget = capValue(source.maxIterations, null);
  const derivedCap = iterationBudget === null
    ? DEFAULT_MAX_FRONTIER_NODES
    : Math.max(1, Math.floor(iterationBudget / 2));
  const runCap = capValue(expansive.maxFrontierNodes, derivedCap);

  const maxChildrenPerNode = capValue(expansive.maxChildrenPerNode, DEFAULT_MAX_CHILDREN_PER_NODE);

  return {
    maxDepth: capValue(expansive.maxDepth, DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH),
    maxChildrenPerNode,
    maxRootChildren: Math.max(maxChildrenPerNode, ROOT_CHILDREN_FLOOR[target]),
    maxFrontierNodes: runCap,
  };
}

function childCapFor(parentId, caps) {
  return parentId === 'root' && Number.isFinite(caps.maxRootChildren) ? caps.maxRootChildren : caps.maxChildrenPerNode;
}

// A review target is a file list, and only those paths can hold a finding. The
// './' prefix and trailing '/' a caller writes carry no meaning, so both sides
// of the comparison are normalized before containment is tested.
function normalizeTargetPath(value) {
  return value.trim().replace(/^\.\//, '').replace(/\/+$/, '');
}

function splitTargetPaths(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return [];
  return value
    .split(/[,\s]+/)
    .map(normalizeTargetPath)
    .filter((entry) => entry !== '');
}

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. One live review pilot ran. Its depth-1 budget check passed, but its stop criterion failed: the root fan-out cap of 6, since raised to 8 for a review seed, pruned a seventh depth-1 node, and three of six lineages stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1. Leaf `frontier_proposal` rows are admitted under relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle checks, in that order. Merge commits are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff`, and index rebuilds are pruned as `generated-artifact`.

### Dimension Coverage

The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name. Each depth band starts from its `budgetSplit` share of `--max-iterations` (0.5, 0.35 and 0.15), plus any share an earlier band left unspent once that band holds no open node, so fund the depth-1 band with at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

### Stop Behavior

A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor.

### Pivot

The divergent review pivot runs only once the frontier is terminal. It seeds from terminal unanswered nodes and routes accepted candidates through admission.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` returns the merged review frontier and runs the review fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one nodes before iteration 1. Each iteration's leaf `frontier_proposal` rows go through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`, which admits each proposal into the node store. It applies the relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle checks in that order, and records the name of the first failing check as the reason for each prune.

### Configuration

`expansiveDepth` in `deep-research-config.json` sets `maxDepth` to 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` to 6, and a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths. `antiConvergence.depthMode` defaults to `default`. The convergence script adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`.

### Stop And Next Focus

While a frontier node is open, a stop candidate, whether `all_questions_answered` or a composite convergence stop, becomes another iteration. Next Focus steers to the shallowest open node. The iteration cap, the anti-convergence floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` and the max-iterations policy decide before the frontier veto, so the frontier never extends a run past them.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages each receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to a lineage only when it is `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers and runs a fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | The `depth_mode` input, the preflight check and the config keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the `expansiveDepth` block. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` | Script | Seeds, admits and closes frontier nodes. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Applies the frontier-open veto and passes `depthMode` into the stop-decision config when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Builds the opt-in `frontier` registry and the question close rule. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` | Script | Passes `depthMode` to lineages only when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` | Script | Merges the lineage frontiers and runs the partition guard. |

| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/frontier-open-veto-holds-soft-stop.md` | Manual playbook | DR-072: an open frontier node turns a composite convergence stop into another iteration. |

+| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
 | `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
 | `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
 | `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the `research/resource-map.md` write; `research.md` still emits. |
 | `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
 | `--restart` | no value | Shorthand that selects `restart`: opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. |

+| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
+| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |
+| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
 | `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports, and the fail-fast on an unsupported pairing, are in the presentation asset. |
 | `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
 | `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.1.0.md:17:- **The two unsupported lifecycle modes are deferred.** When a research packet already existed, the workflow asked operators how to continue. The choices were `resume` the same run or start a fresh run. The other two were `fork` into a sibling run history and `completed-continue` to reopen a finished run after snapshotting its synthesis. The first two choices worked. `fork` and `completed-continue` had no workflow wiring. They did what `resume` did and quietly kept the old session id. So operators who thought they had branched a research run history overwrote the original. `SKILL.md`, `README.md`, both loop-protocol references and both command workflows now mark the modes as deferred. So do the Claude Code and Codex agent definitions. The normal happy path stays the same. The visible choices are `new`, `resume` and `restart`. The v1.5.0.0 changelog gained an append-only "Update (post-release)" block that points at this release.

Appends `deep_research.run_extended` through the append gateway, which projects an `extended` row into the state log, and only then replaces the config with `status: "initialized"`, `lineageMode: "completed-continue"` and `continuedFromRun`. A failed append leaves the run complete and untouched.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:142:The extended row carries `{"type":"event","event":"extended","sessionId","generation","lineageMode":"completed-continue","continuedFromRun","extension","timestamp"}`, where `extension` holds `addedIterations`, `priorMaxIterations`, `newMaxIterations`, `priorMaxDurationMinutes`, `newMaxDurationMinutes` and `elapsedMinutesAtExtension`. It has no `archivedPath` or `parentSessionId`, so the field rule below covers `resumed` and `restarted` only.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && rg -n "three supported modes|four supported modes|three supported|completed-continue.*deferred" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop .skilled/commands/deep .skilled/agents 2>/dev/null | head -20
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.3.1.0.md:23:- **The interactive lifecycle gate offers only working choices.** Choose `resume`, `restart` or `cancel`. `fork` and `completed-continue` are deferred.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.3.1.0.md:40:When an operator re-entered a review session, the interactive workflow offered `resume` (continue where you left off) and `restart`. It also offered `fork` (start a sibling run history). It offered `completed-continue` as well (reopen a finished run after snapshotting its synthesis). Only `resume` and `restart` were implemented. Selecting either unsupported mode did not branch the run. The state log silently kept the same session ID. That left earlier work open to overwrite. `fork` and `completed-continue` are now explicitly deferred. The skill guidance, workflows and runtime mirrors all mark both modes as deferred. The gate offers `resume`, `restart` or `cancel`. `restart` starts a new generation. Both paths append a typed event to the state log. That event carries the run history fields. They are session ID, parent session ID, run history mode, continued-from-run, generation, archive path and timestamp.

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Reach for the hub when a request needs more than one round. Use the research mode for a topic that spans several sources, where each round should shape the next query. Use the review mode for a multi-pass audit of a spec folder or a file set. Use it for a release-readiness verdict. Both modes fit an unattended run, because each keeps its state on disk and checks convergence after every pass.

Use `@context` for a one-shot lookup and `/speckit:plan` for implementation planning. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`. A planning council is a separate agent, reached through `/speckit:ai-council`. It is not a mode of this hub.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec packets, validation and memory continuity that both modes read and write. |
| `sk-code` | Its code-review mode handles a single-pass review. The `deep-review/` packet runs the multi-pass loop. |

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The compiled route returns `"action":"defer"` | The prompt names no mode, because `routerPolicy.defaultMode` is `null` | Add a mode word such as `deep research` or `deep review`. A mode hint works too. |
| The loop did not start | The mode was dispatched outside its command, so no workflow owned the state | Run `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. The command YAML owns state, dispatch and convergence. |
| The drift check reports `registry packet absent from hub index` | This README no longer names a packet folder that `mode-registry.json` registers | Add the folder name with a trailing slash to the README |

---

## 6. VERIFICATION

The parent-skill check validates the registry shape, the tool-surface union, the hub-router consistency and the ROUTER contract in one pass.

```bash
node .skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop
```

Expected output: `PASS:` lines for the checked invariants, with `INFO:` lines where the hub declares no surface packets. The summary reads `all hard invariants passed, 0 warnings`. Exit code 0.

```bash
node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/check-documentation-drift.cjs
```

Expected output: a JSON report whose `errors` array is empty. Exit code 0. The check confirms that every registered packet appears in this README with a trailing slash and that every benchmark report folder is indexed. It also fails on a broken relative link in the hub, packet, runtime and report docs.

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/scripts/validate_document.py .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md --type readme
```

Expected output: `✅ VALID` and `Total issues: 0`.

on_halt:
          action: "Every terminal halt after the acquire runs step_release_lock before it returns control"
          command: "node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/loop-lock.cjs release --lock-path {state_paths.lock_file} --owner-pid {captured_owner_pid} --nonce {captured_acquire_nonce}; failures here are logged but do not block the halt"
        on_cancel:
          action: "Release the lock on user cancel or pause-sentinel detection before sentinel processing"
          command: "node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/loop-lock.cjs release --lock-path {state_paths.lock_file} --owner-pid {captured_owner_pid} --nonce {captured_acquire_nonce}; cancel completes regardless of cleanup result"
        on_workflow_exit:
          action: "Release the lock as the final step of any workflow exit path (success or failure)"
          command: "node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/loop-lock.cjs release --lock-path {state_paths.lock_file} --owner-pid {captured_owner_pid} --nonce {captured_acquire_nonce}; idempotent, safe to invoke when already released"

      step_extend_completed_run:
        action: "Extend a completed run in place, under the lock and before classification, so the classifier sees a resumable run"
        skip_when: "lineage_mode != 'completed-continue'"
        dry_run_boundary: "state-mutation"
        note: |
          --extend=N continues a finished run in the same session and generation. The
          duration clock counts wall-clock time since createdAt, idle time included, so the
          new budget starts from whichever is later, the old budget or the time already
          spent, and createdAt is never re-stamped. The event is appended before the
          config is replaced, so a failed append leaves the run complete and untouched.
        command: |
          GATEWAY=".skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/append-mode-event.cjs"
          [ -f "$GATEWAY" ] || GATEWAY="scripts/append-mode-event.cjs"
          WORK_DIR="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/deep-research-extend.XXXXXX")"
          node -e '
            const fs = require("fs"), crypto = require("crypto"), path = require("path");
            const [configPath, stateLogPath, inboxPath, extendRaw, extendMinutesRaw, workDir] = process.argv.slice(1);
            const fail = (message) => { console.error(message); process.exit(2); };
            const config = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(configPath, "utf8"));
            // The preflight check refuses these first; this keeps a bad value from reaching the config.
            if (!/^[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(String(extendRaw))) {
              fail("--extend needs a positive integer, and got " + JSON.stringify(String(extendRaw)) + ".");
            }
            const minutesRaw = String(extendMinutesRaw);
            if (minutesRaw !== "" && !minutesRaw.startsWith("{") && !/^[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(minutesRaw)) {
              fail("--extend-minutes needs a positive integer, and got " + JSON.stringify(minutesRaw) + ".");
            }
            if (config.status !== "complete") {
              fail("--extend needs a completed run, and this run has status " + config.status + ". Use --lineage-mode=resume to continue an unfinished run.");
            }
            const stateBytes = fs.readFileSync(stateLogPath);
            const rows = stateBytes.toString("utf8").split("\n").filter(Boolean).flatMap((line) => {
              try { return [JSON.parse(line)]; } catch { return []; }
            });
            // One run per iteration number, error and timeout rows included. The integer
            // iteration counts first and a numeric run is the fallback, as the state reader
            // counts them, because a leaf may write its run id string in run.
            const continuedFromRun = new Set(rows
              .filter((row) => row && row.type === "iteration")
              .map((row) => (Number.isInteger(row.iteration) ? row.iteration : Number(row.run)))
              .filter((run) => Number.isInteger(run))).size;
            const addedIterations = Number(extendRaw);
            const priorMaxIterations = Number(config.maxIterations) || 0;
            const priorMaxDurationMinutes = Number(config.maxDurationMinutes) || 0;
            const createdMs = Date.parse(config.createdAt);
            const elapsedMinutesAtExtension = Number.isFinite(createdMs)
              ? Math.max(0, Math.floor((Date.now() - createdMs) / 60000))
              : 0;
            // An unset flag renders as an empty or unexpanded value, never as digits.
            const hasMinutes = /^[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(String(extendMinutesRaw));
            const addedMinutes = hasMinutes ? Number(extendMinutesRaw) : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            // A run with no duration budget keeps none unless --extend-minutes asks for one.
            const newMaxDurationMinutes = priorMaxDurationMinutes > 0 || hasMinutes
              ? Math.max(priorMaxDurationMinutes, elapsedMinutesAtExtension) + addedMinutes
              : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            const newMaxIterations = continuedFromRun + addedIterations;
            const lineage = config.lineage || {};
            const sessionId = lineage.sessionId;
            const lineageId = lineage.parentSessionId || sessionId;
            const generation = Number(lineage.generation) || 1;
            const event = {
              stem: "deep_research.run_extended",
              scope: { runId: sessionId, lineageId },
              data: {
                priorTailDigest: crypto.createHash("sha256").update(stateBytes).digest("hex"),
                sessionId,
                generation,
                continuedFromRun,
                extension: {
                  addedIterations,
                  priorMaxIterations,
                  newMaxIterations,
                  priorMaxDurationMinutes,
                  newMaxDurationMinutes,
                  elapsedMinutesAtExtension,
                },
              },
            };
            const next = {
              ...config,
              status: "initialized",
              maxIterations: newMaxIterations,
              maxDurationMinutes: newMaxDurationMinutes,
              lineage: { ...lineage, lineageMode: "completed-continue", continuedFromRun },
            };
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "event.json"), JSON.stringify(event));
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "config.json"), JSON.stringify(next, null, 2) + "\n");
            let pendingInbox = 0;
            try {
              pendingInbox = fs.readFileSync(inboxPath, "utf8").split("\n").filter((line) => line.trim() !== "").length;
            } catch (error) {
              if (error.code !== "ENOENT") throw error;
            }
            // Config creation writes the stop policy at the top level, beside maxIterations.
            const stopPolicy = config.stopPolicy || "convergence";
            const warning = stopPolicy === "convergence" && pendingInbox === 0
              ? "This run stops on convergence and has no pending inbox question, so it may re-stop on all_questions_answered right away. Pass --convergence-mode=off, use stopPolicy max-iterations, or add questions to research/inbox.jsonl to keep it running."
              : null;
            console.log(JSON.stringify({ continuedFromRun, newMaxIterations, newMaxDurationMinutes, warning }));
          ' "{state_paths.config}" "{state_paths.state_log}" "{state_paths.packet_dir}/inbox.jsonl" "{extend}" "{extend_minutes}" "$WORK_DIR" > "$WORK_DIR/result.json" || { rm -rf "$WORK_DIR"; exit 2; }
          node "$GATEWAY" --mode research --run-directory "$(dirname "{state_paths.state_log}")" --event-json "$WORK_DIR/event.json" || { rm -rf "$WORK_DIR"; exit 1; }
          mv "$WORK_DIR/config.json" "{state_paths.config}" || { rm -rf "$WORK_DIR"; exit 1; }
          cat "$WORK_DIR/result.json"
          rm -rf "$WORK_DIR"
        outputs:
          - continued_from_run: "continuedFromRun from the JSON output"
          - max_iterations: "newMaxIterations from the JSON output; it replaces the command default so the loop cap and the convergence script read the same ceiling"
          - extension_pending: "true"
          - extension_warning: "warning from the JSON output, null when none"
        on_warning: "When extension_warning is not null, print it and continue. It never refuses the extension."
        on_fail: "Exit 2 is a usage error, such as a run that is not complete: halt with that message through step_release_lock and leave the config untouched. Any other failure halts with STATUS=FAIL through step_release_lock."

      step_snapshot_extended_synthesis:
        action: "Keep the prior synthesis before the extension's first dispatch, so re-synthesis cannot overwrite it"
        skip_when: "extension_pending != true"
        dry_run_boundary: "state-mutation"
        command: |
          GENERATION="$(node -p 'const c = JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(process.argv[1], "utf8")); Number((c.lineage || c).generation) || 1' "{state_paths.config}")" || exit 1
          SNAPSHOT="{state_paths.packet_dir}/synthesis-v${GENERATION}-r{continued_from_run}.md"
          if [ -e "$SNAPSHOT" ]; then
            echo "The synthesis snapshot $SNAPSHOT is already there." >&2
            exit 1
          fi
          if [ -f "{state_paths.research_output}" ]; then
            cp "{state_paths.research_output}" "$SNAPSHOT"
          fi
        note: "Keyed by generation and continuedFromRun, so a second extension of the same generation writes its own snapshot."
        on_fail: "Halt with STATUS=FAIL through step_release_lock. Never overwrite an existing snapshot."

      step_classify_session:
        action: "Classify the session before writing files"
        inspect:
          - "{state_paths.config}"
          - "{state_paths.state_log}"
          - "{state_paths.strategy}"
        classify:

  step_reject_extend_with_fanout:
    description: "Refuse --extend on a fan-out config before any lineage spawns"
    skip_when: "lineage_mode != 'completed-continue' or config.fanout is absent"
    instructions: |
      A fan-out run jumps from step_fanout_spawn to phase_synthesis and never
      reaches the lock, the pre-classify extension step or the classifier, so an
      extension there would change nothing and report no error. Refuse the
      combination here, before step_fanout_spawn, so no lineage spawns.
    halt: true
    status: "FAIL"
    message: "Usage error (extend-with-fanout): --extend extends a single-executor research run only. This config has fanout executors, so nothing was spawned and the run is unchanged."

  step_frontier_seed:
    description: "Seed the depth-1 frontier before any fan-out lineage spawns"
    enabled_when: "depth_mode == 'expansive'"
    skip_when: "config.fanout is absent"
    dry_run_boundary: "state-mutation"
    instructions: |
      Fan-out jumps from step_fanout_spawn to phase_synthesis and never reaches
      phase_init, so the seed runs here or not at all. fanout-run.cjs then splits
      the seeded depth-1 nodes round-robin across the lineages.

  # ════════
  # PHASE 1: INITIALIZATION
  # ════════
  phase_init:
    purpose: Create all state files for the research session
    steps:

      step_preflight_contract:
        action: "Validate required setup bindings and executable workflow contract before any file writes"
        verify:
          - required_values_present: [research_topic, spec_folder, lineage_mode, max_iterations, convergence_threshold, convergence_mode, depth_mode, stop_policy]
          - dry_run_normalized: "Boolean true or false before workflow loading; absent flag resolves to false."
          - spec_folder_is_within: "specs/ or .opencode/specs/ (canonicalize alias roots before validation)"
          - unresolved_placeholders: "FAIL if any user-facing template references placeholders not produced by earlier steps"
          - tool_contract: "FAIL if executable steps reference tools outside the command allowlist"
          - extend_values: "When extend is bound it must be a positive integer (digits only, no sign, at least 1), and extend_minutes when bound likewise. extend_minutes without extend, or extend with a lineage_mode other than auto or completed-continue, is a usage error. Each fails here, before step_acquire_lock, with a usage error that names the flag."
        on_fail: "Halt with STATUS=FAIL and report the missing binding or unsupported contract reference"

      step_create_directories:
        action: "Create spec folder and research packet directories if they don't exist"
        dry_run_boundary: "state-mutation"
        command: "mkdir -p {state_paths.prompt_dir} {state_paths.iteration_dir} {state_paths.delta_dir}"

      step_detect_resource_map:
        action: "Detect resource-map.md before config creation so init can persist the flag and seed Known Context"
        inspect:
          - "{spec_folder}/resource-map.md"
        classify:
          present: "resource-map.md exists at init"
          absent: "resource-map.md does not exist at init"
        outputs:
          - resource_map_present: "Boolean true when {spec_folder}/resource-map.md exists, else false"
          - resource_map_known_context: "When present: summarize the map by section with entry counts across READMEs, Documents, Commands, Agents, Skills, Specs, Scripts, Tests, Config, and Meta, plus a one-line theme summary for each section. When absent: 'resource-map.md not present; skipping coverage gate'."

      step_resolve_canonical_names:
        action: "Resolve canonical research packet artifact names before classification"
        inspect_legacy:
          - "{state_paths.packet_dir}/config.json"
          - "{state_paths.packet_dir}/state.jsonl"
          - "{state_paths.packet_dir}/strategy.md"

# Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no depth field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flag is present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow reads `--depth-mode` and binds it as `depth_mode`. The runtime acts only on the value `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`, and `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to fan-out lineages only when the value is `expansive`. A default run carries no depth key in either place.

### Packet Behavior

The expansive behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [expansive depth mode for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md) and [expansive depth mode for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md).

---

|---|---|---|

| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row for review. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Adds `depthMode` to the stop-decision config only when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` | Script | Passes `depthMode` to lineages only when expansive. |

# Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` and `--extend-minutes=M` are command-level options on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. They reopen a completed run in place. The flags are not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without them. The extension behavior lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no extension field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flags are present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow binds `--extend=N` as `extend` with lineage mode `completed-continue`, and `--extend-minutes=M` as `extend_minutes`. Both take positive integers, and `--extend-minutes` is valid only with `--extend`. The extension step sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N and keeps the session and generation. A config with fan-out executors is refused before any lineage spawns.

### Packet Behavior

The extension behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [completed-run extension for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md) and [completed-run extension for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md).

---

|---|---|---|

| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--extend` and `--extend-minutes` flag rows for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--extend` and `--extend-minutes` flag rows for review. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | `step_reject_extend_with_fanout` and `step_extend_completed_run` for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Workflow | `step_reject_extend_with_fanout` and `step_extend_completed_run` for review. |

| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-review-run.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-070: `--extend=N` on a completed review run. |

# Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification (system-deep-loop)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`system-deep-loop` is a registry-driven, invokable hub. `mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth for its two `workflowMode` packets; the hub classifies a request, resolves the mode through the registry, and loads `registry[mode].packet` without re-deriving the mapping or holding per-mode logic itself.

The `/deep:*` commands and native agent types are complementary surfaces that reach the same packets through their own static routers or agent definitions — they do not bypass or duplicate the registry.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Three-Tier Discriminator

Every packet carries `workflowMode` (the public mode key: `research`, `review`), `runtimeLoopType` (the graph-backed convergence key consumed by `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`, validated against active `research`/`review`), and `backendKind` (`runtime-loop-type` for research/review).

### Intent Classification, Not Keyed Resource Discovery

The advisor routes any deep-loop query to the single identity `system-deep-loop`; the hub then classifies intent to a `workflowMode` (a mode hint like `"research: ..."` overrides), guards `mode-registry.json`, and loads the resolved packet. It omits `discover_markdown_resources` and never selects hub-level `references/<key>/` or `assets/<key>/` resources by runtime key. A second-layer surface router at the root `ROUTER.md` (mirroring the equivalent surfaces in `sk-prompt` and `sk-code`) maps a resolved mode's intent to packet-qualified child leaves for router-replay benchmarking; it never re-decides the mode and is not itself a runtime discovery surface.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/SKILL.md` | Shared | States the registry-driven classification contract and names the three discriminator keys as terms. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` | Shared | Declarative registry for both `workflowMode` packets. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/ROUTER.md` | Shared | Second-layer intent-to-leaf mapping consumed by router-replay benchmarking, and the three-tier discriminator section with its never-infer rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs` | Automated test | Structural hub conformance, including the tool-surface-union invariant this hub's broad `allowed-tools` grant satisfies. |

---

## 4. SOURCE METADATA

- Group: Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification
- Canonical catalog source: `feature-catalog.md`
- Feature file path: `deep-loop-mode-classification/deep-loop-mode-classification.md`

# system-deep-loop: Feature Catalog

This catalog inventories the live `system-deep-loop` hub surface. The hub classifies a request into one of two `workflowMode` packets (`research`, `review`) through a three-tier discriminator, holding no per-mode logic itself. A default-on, flag-gated compiled-routing fast path can resolve the same decision ahead of this registry-driven classification without changing what it resolves to.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the current-state inventory for the `system-deep-loop` hub. The hub classifies intent and loads the resolved packet; the `/deep:*` commands and native agent types are complementary surfaces that reach the same packets through their own static routers.

---

## 2. DEEP-LOOP MODE ROUTING

### Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification

#### Description

`mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth for the hub's two `workflowMode` packets, each carrying a `workflowMode`/`runtimeLoopType`/`backendKind` three-tier discriminator.

#### Current Reality

`runtimeLoopType` is the graph-backed convergence key consumed by `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`; `backendKind` is `runtime-loop-type` (research/review). A second-layer surface router at the root `ROUTER.md` supports router-replay benchmarking without re-deciding the mode.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/deep-loop-mode-classification.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/deep-loop-mode-classification.md) for the full discriminator and source anchors.

---

### Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

#### Description

The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.

#### How It Works

`mode-registry.json` has no depth field, and the runtime acts only on the expansive value. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)

#### Description

The --extend=N and --extend-minutes=M flags on /deep:research and /deep:review reopen a completed single-executor run in place, and leave the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.

#### How It Works

`mode-registry.json` has no extension field. The extension sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, keeps the session and generation, and is refused with fan-out. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

## 3. FAN-OUT WRITE CONTAINMENT

### Fan-Out Write Containment

#### Description

How a fan-out keeps a dispatched lane's writes inside its own directory, and why the remedy is preservation rather than reversion.

#### Current Reality

Preservation is the default remedy and restore is opt-in per run. Every lane runs in the shared checkout, so the containment guard watches the checkout itself: an out-of-scope write stays on disk and is reported as an advisory or a violation. A burst of newly dirty paths between heartbeats proves a second writer and latches preservation even when restore was requested.

#### Source Files

See [fanout-write-containment/fanout-write-containment.md](fanout-write-containment/fanout-write-containment.md).

---

## 4. COMPILED ROUTING

### Compiled Routing And Legacy Fallback

#### Description

A default-on, flag-gated, additive directive in `system-deep-loop`'s `ROUTER.md` asks the compiled per-hub router contract to resolve the mode before falling through to the deep-loop mode classification above.

#### Current Reality

The directive is on by default for `system-deep-loop`, one of the seven activated hubs: with `SPECKIT_COMPILED_ROUTING` unset, `node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "<task>"` returns the authoritative decision and the hub follows it directly. Setting `SPECKIT_COMPILED_ROUTING=0` is the explicit kill-switch that forces legacy `mode-registry.json` classification; any error or a `{"servingAuthority":"legacy"}` sentinel also leaves routing unchanged.

#### Source Files

See [`compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback/compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback.md`](compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback/compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback.md) for resolution order, the tri-state flag, and serving-status anchors.

    section: Known Context

      label: Track

  dimensions:
    - id: correctness
      priority: 1
      label: Correctness
      checks: Validate logic, invariants, state transitions, edge cases, and behavior against observable intent.
      requiredForSeverityCoverage: true
    - id: security
      priority: 2
      label: Security
      checks: Validate trust boundaries, authz/authn behavior, input handling, secrets exposure, and exploit paths.
      requiredForSeverityCoverage: true
    - id: traceability
      priority: 3
      label: Traceability
      checks: Validate spec alignment, completeness, checklist evidence, and cross-reference integrity across linked artifacts.
      requiredForSeverityCoverage: false
    - id: maintainability
      priority: 4
      label: Maintainability
      checks: Validate codebase patterns, documentation quality, clarity, and ease of safe follow-on changes.
      requiredForSeverityCoverage: false

  # A configured name outside the four above is a custom dimension. It is tracked
  # and covered like the others, ordered after them, and judged by its own name.
  customDimensionRule:
    priority: after-canonical
    order: configured
    checks: Review the target for the property the configured name states, under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine.
    requiredForSeverityCoverage: false

  # Severity contract stays stable across review outputs.
  severities:
    - id: P0
      weight: 10.0
      label: Blocker
      requiresFileLineEvidence: true
    - id: P1
      weight: 5.0
      label: Required
      requiresFileLineEvidence: true
    - id: P2
      weight: 1.0
      label: Suggestion
      requiresFileLineEvidence: true

  # Verdicts collapse PASS WITH NOTES into PASS + hasAdvisories metadata.
  # A run that stops before any iteration fails coverage and so ends FAIL; its report routes to another /deep:review, not /speckit:plan.
  verdicts:
    - id: FAIL
      label: Fail
      condition: "activeP0 > 0 OR any required quality gate fails"
      nextCommand: /speckit:plan
    - id: CONDITIONAL
      label: Conditional
      condition: "activeP0 == 0 AND no required quality gate fails AND (activeP1 > 0 OR open search debt > 0)"
      nextCommand: /speckit:plan
    - id: PASS
      label: Pass
      condition: "activeP0 == 0 AND activeP1 == 0 AND no open search debt"
      hasAdvisoriesMetadata: "Set hasAdvisories=true when activeP2 > 0."
      nextCommand: /create:changelog

  # Binary gates applied after convergence math votes STOP.
  qualityGates:
    - id: evidence
      label: Evidence
      binary: true
      combines:
        - evidence-completeness
        - no-inference-only
      rule: Every active finding must be backed by concrete file:line evidence and may not rely only on inference.
    - id: scope
      label: Scope
      binary: true
      combines:
        - scope-alignment
      rule: Reviewed files, targets, and conclusions must stay inside the declared review scope.

143:### Review Dimensions
145:The four primary review dimensions (configured in `assets/review-mode-contract.yaml`; configure them by the id in parentheses, because any other name counts as a custom dimension):
147:| Dimension | Focus | Key Questions |
175:The scale has exactly three tiers, and no fourth is reserved.
176:A severity outside P0/P1/P2 is not a fourth tier. That covers a `P3` inherited from an older four-level convention, and any unlisted word.
241:- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
245:- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.
254:2. Review one dimension per iteration and write findings to `iteration-NNN.md`.
285:3. **All dimensions covered with P0 findings remaining**, Human sign-off required before shipping.
325:- Every configured dimension plus the required traceability protocols (`spec_code`, `checklist_evidence`) has at least one full iteration of coverage.
339:8. Dimension and protocol coverage stability.
=== research SKILL
89:### Expansive Depth Mode
91:- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.
92:- `step_frontier_seed` writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`, after `step_create_config`, or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run.
93:- `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Admitted nodes land in `research/inbox.jsonl` with `origin: "frontier"`.
94:- Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>`.
95:- After the `minIterations` floor an open or active node turns a soft stop into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never creates a stop and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`.
134:| `completed-continue` | Set by `--extend=N`: reopen a completed run in the same session and generation, raise the iteration cap to the runs so far plus N, and append a typed `run_extended` event |
317:| `/speckit:resume` | Canonical recovery surface before resuming/extending a packet |

<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

> every row below is `Met`, `Waived` or `Superseded`. A `Waived` or `Superseded`
> row MUST name an ADR that exists in `decision-record.md`.

### Status values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `Met` | Verified. The Verification cell names evidence that was actually observed. |
| `Unmet` | Not yet satisfied. Blocks closure. |
| `Waived` | Deliberately not pursued. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |
| `Superseded` | Replaced by a different criterion or decision. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |

### Waiver cell

Write `-` when the row is `Met` or `Unmet`. Write `ADR-NNN` when the row is
`Waived` or `Superseded`, naming a decision record that exists in
`decision-record.md`. A waiver naming an ADR that is not there fails validation:
the point of a waiver is that someone recorded the reasoning, so an unbacked
waiver is treated as an unmet criterion rather than as a pass.
<!-- /ANCHOR:criteria -->

**Closeable:** No

Yes. AC-014 is met under the amended criterion (decision-record Q003) on the run-2 pilot evidence.

100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. 105:| `--review-focus` | `"TEXT"` | Optional operator focus that every iteration covers in addition to its dimension, in single-executor and fan-out runs alike. It is cut to one line of at most 1000 characters, stored as `reviewFocus` in the config and shown in the review report. |
107:| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |
