===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.0.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0, completed runs can be extended"
description: "Completed deep-research and deep-review runs take --extend=N, which adds iterations in the same session and keeps the prior synthesis or report as a snapshot."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.0.0"
  - "completed run extension"
  - "run_extended ledger event"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.0.0
---
# v2.1.0.0, Completed Runs Can Be Extended

A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to get more iterations. `--extend=N` reopens the finished run in place. The same session and generation carry on. The earlier results stay on record.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Completed runs take `--extend=N`.** `/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` add N iterations to a completed run in the same session and generation.
- **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** Three cases stop with a named error. They are a run that is not complete, a fan-out config and a value that is not a positive integer.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Without `--extend`, nothing changes.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/001-completed-run-extension`
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.1.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0, source diversity reads derived source classes"
description: "Research SOURCE nodes get a quality_class derived from their path when they are stored, so source diversity scores real classes. A question answered only from code still blocks."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.1.0"
  - "source diversity quality class"
  - "derived quality_class"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.1.0
---
# v2.1.1.0, Source Diversity Reads Derived Source Classes

Source diversity could not score real classes, because no producer set `quality_class`. Research SOURCE nodes now get a class when they are stored. The diversity signal reads what the sources are.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Research sources are classified when stored.** `upsert.cjs` sets `quality_class` on each research SOURCE node from its path first, then its label, then its id. A value the leaf supplies is overwritten.
- **Source diversity has real values on normal runs.** The signal counts the distinct classes a question's findings cite. It no longer moves only on fixtures that typed a class in.
- **A code-only question still blocks.** A question answered only from code files scores 1.0 and stops at `source_diversity_guard`, by design. Code plus its tests scores 2.0 and passes.
- **The threshold is unchanged.** The 1.5 threshold and the averaging rule did not change.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/007-source-diversity-quality-class`
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.2.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0, merged fan-out reviews keep their coverage"
description: "A merged two-lineage review fan-out keeps the coverage its lineages recorded: dimension, traceability, search, candidate, divergence and ruled-out surfaces are carried through the merge."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.2.0"
  - "fan-out review coverage merge"
  - "divergenceByLineage"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.2.0
---
# v2.1.2.0, Merged Fan-Out Reviews Keep Their Coverage

A review that fans out to several lineages used to lose the coverage each lineage had recorded when the results were merged. The merge now carries those surfaces through, so the merged review shows what every lineage covered.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Dimension coverage is a union.** `dimensionCoverage` holds the dimensions every lineage covered, with sorted keys.
- **Traceability keeps the strictest status.** Per protocol, `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. An undecided status never replaces a decided one.
- **Search and candidate coverage are merged.** `searchCoverage` holds sorted unions. Each `candidateCoverage` bucket lists the lineages that reported it.
- **Divergence stays per lineage.** `divergenceByLineage` keeps each lineage's pivot state under its label. No merged `divergence` is written, because a merged current focus would be invented.
- **Ruled-out candidates name their lineage.** Each entry carries a `lineage` field and is deduplicated on lineage and id.
- **The merge is order-independent.** Lineages are read in label order, so the same lineages give the same merged result whatever order they arrive in.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. This release adds the data only. No stop decision reads these surfaces yet.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/009-fanout-review-coverage-merge`
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.3.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.3.0, free text no longer steers Next Focus"
description: "Research and review Next Focus follow open questions and the leaf's recommendation, not status sentences or answered questions. Normal runs change with no flag."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.3.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.3.0"
  - "next focus free text"
  - "carried-forward open questions"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.3.0
---

# v2.1.3.0, Free Text No Longer Steers Next Focus

Next Focus could follow a sentence that only reported progress. A run then spent a round on a settled question. Next Focus now follows open questions and the latest leaf's recommendation.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research carries forward only open questions. Status sentences and answered questions no longer return.
- Research reads the latest leaf's `Recommended Next Focus`. It leads while a machine question is open, unless that focus is already answered or rejected.
- Review names the first uncovered dimension. Leaf free text that names no dimension is ignored.
- Imperative items, such as `Investigate the review twin`, no longer carry forward. The leaf's recommendation can still carry one into Next Focus.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases failed before the change and pass after it. The three reducer and continuity suites pass, and every existing case is kept.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Normal research and review runs use the new focus rules with no flag.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/004-focus-hijack-fix`
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.4.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0, divergent pivots get one quorum retry"
description: "A divergent pivot gets three council seats per attempt and one retry when quorum misses. A run now uses at most 18 seat outputs."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.4.0"
  - "divergent pivot quorum retry"
  - "maxCouncilSeatOutputs"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.4.0
---

# v2.1.4.0, Divergent Pivots Get One Quorum Retry

A divergent pivot ended on its first quorum miss. One missed seat could end the pivot. A pivot now gets one retry before it stops.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` now means three per council attempt.
- A first-attempt quorum miss gets one retry.
- A second miss still ends the pivot. No third attempt opens.
- A later pivot is no longer refused because earlier pivots used seats.
- A run uses at most 18 seat outputs. That is three pivots with six seats each.
- Research and review pivots follow the same rule.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Eleven new cases cover the seat budget, the retry and the attempt identity. The divergent pivot suites pass. Attempt 1 keeps its existing paths and event ids.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Pivots written before this change still load. A first-attempt quorum miss now runs one more council round.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/012-pivot-hygiene`
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.5.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0, landed iterations are not dispatched twice"
description: "A research or review iteration that already landed is no longer dispatched again after three verify failures. Every other failure still gets one redispatch."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.5.0"
  - "landed iteration redispatch"
  - "executor_missing redispatch"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.5.0
---

# v2.1.5.0, Landed Iterations Are Not Dispatched Twice

A research or review iteration could be dispatched again after it had landed. The second worker would find its files written. It would then refuse to overwrite them. The loop waited on a run that could not succeed.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Three verify failures skip the redispatch. They are `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` and `ledger_backing_missing`.
- Those failures appear only after the iteration's narrative, state record and delta exist.
- Research and review follow the same rule.
- Every other failure still gets one redispatch, including route proof.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases cover the landed-iteration skip across the verify, counter and reducer suites. Those suites pass. The existing append-only redispatch case still passes.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Expect one dispatch fewer for these three failures.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration`

# v2.1.6.0, Research Runs Use the Configured Claude Config Dir

A research run on Claude ignored the config dir the operator set. It ran under whichever account the shell had. Research now honors the configured dir.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research accepts `--claude-config-dir` on `cli-claude-code` runs.
- The dir is checked before the prompt is read. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused.
- Claude runs under the configured dir. It overrides an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- The audit record names the dir the run used.
- A run that names no dir behaves as before.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

New cases cover the flag, its validation and the audit record. All ten dispatch cases pass in a detached run. Inside a Claude session, two skip under the Claude ancestry guard.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Set `configDir` in the run's executor config to pin the account. An inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` still reaches a research run that names no dir. Stripping it would change every default run, so it stays as it is.

&nbsp;

- A kill switch in `hook-flags.env` turns a Vibe part off.
- Hooks run from a symlinked checkout.

</details>

&nbsp;

## Deep Loop

Deep loops run research and review passes on their own until their evidence says stop. Their verdicts and stop calls now come from scripts instead of judgment, so a rerun on the same state always agrees. The AI Council also moves out into an agent of its own.

#### The AI Council Is Its Own Agent <a id="c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent"></a>

> [!WARNING]
> `/deep:ai-council` is gone. Run `/speckit:ai-council` instead.

- It binds the spec folder and dispatches `@ai-council` once.
- The agent runs three rounds by default.
- It writes only under the packet's `ai-council/` folder.

#### A Review Fails While a Required Check Fails <a id="c-a-review-fails-while-a-required-check-fails"></a>

- A review returns FAIL when a P0 is open or a required check fails.
- The required checks are evidence, scope and coverage.
- Scripts compute the checks and the verdict.
- Coverage reads every shape a review records.
- The report carries the counts the verdict used.
- Open search debt makes a review CONDITIONAL.

#### Loops Stop the Same Way Every Time <a id="c-loops-stop-the-same-way-every-time"></a>

- `convergence.cjs --stop-decision` makes the stop call as one `stop_decision` object.
- The review vote follows your `convergenceThreshold`.
- A resume rebuilds its counts from the state log.
- Three failed passes in a row end the loop with reason `error`.
- `--restart` and `--dry-run` work on research and review.
- `maxDurationMinutes` ends a run on time.
- A review fan-out takes `--review-target`.
- Each deep command runs from one workflow file.

<details>
<summary><b>Smaller fixes</b></summary>

- Vibe runs bill your Vibe plan through your `vibe --setup` sign-in.
- A quota refusal counts as `quota`, not a crash.
- `fanout-run.cjs` refuses a one-lineage run.
- A pass with no recorded executor fails as `executor_missing`.
- Deep-loop scripts no longer hang on an open stdin.
- Deep-loop saves write continuity.
- One DeepSeek Harness lineage runs through `dispatch-iteration.cjs` with `--spec-folder`.
- A review that never ran says so.

301:`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<one new question, not a restatement of an existing one>","scope":"<path, module or area>","evidence":"<file:line or URL that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`

title: "Review minIterations floor"
description: "A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so a review never stops before the floor."
trigger_phrases:

  - "minIterationsNotReached"
  - "review iteration floor"
  - "antiConvergence minIterations review"
version: 1.0.2.0
---

# Review minIterations floor

## 1. OVERVIEW

The review convergence check holds a stop below the configured floor. `config.minIterations` when present, else `antiConvergence.minIterations`, sets the floor, capped at `maxIterations`; the shipped default is 2. The floor applies to research and review alike.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Floor Check

`runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with the reason `minIterationsNotReached` while the iteration count is below the floor. A stop candidate below the floor never ends the review.

### Stop Order

The review command's `step_check_convergence` places the floor in the fixed stop order after the candidate line and before the gates, so the floor is checked before any gate can end the run.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | The floor check that returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The floor's position in the fixed stop order of `step_check_convergence`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.minIterations`, default 2. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/stop-decision.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The review floor case: `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | A configured floor above 2. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-stop-path-replay.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The shipped floor replays unchanged. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/review-min-iterations-floor.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-072: a review run holds below its minIterations floor. |

=== custom dims
---
title: "Custom review dimensions"
description: "A review run accepts configured custom dimensions after the four canonical ones, tracks them in dimension coverage, and lets a covered custom dimension reach the all-dimensions-clean stop."
trigger_phrases:
  - "custom review dimension"
  - "custom dimension review"
  - "configured review dimension"
  - "review dimension coverage custom"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |

# State Format Reference

Live state packet hub for deep-research files, mutability, and routed state references.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### Purpose

Summarize the live deep-research packet files, mutability rules, and focused state references without carrying every JSON shape inline.

### When to Use

Load this hub when navigating packet files, deciding file ownership, or choosing which detailed state reference to load next.

### Routed Details

- `state-jsonl.md` for config, iteration, event, lifecycle, graph, and blocked-stop records.
- `state-outputs.md` for strategy, iteration markdown, `research.md`, dashboard, and resource-map output.
- `state-reducer-registry.md` for reducer ownership, findings registry, validation, reconstruction, and file protection.

For iterative code review state, use `deep-review`. Review-mode state is not part of this skill's live state contract.

### Packet Summary

The deep-research loop persists continuity in packet files so each iteration can run with fresh context.

| File | Format | Purpose | Mutability |
|------|--------|---------|------------|
| `deep-research-config.json` | JSON | Loop parameters and lineage | Created at init; read-only after |
| `deep-research-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Append-only structured log | Append-only |
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Markdown | Current research plan and next focus | Reducer-managed sections |
| `deep-research-findings-registry.json` / `findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned findings and question state | Auto-generated |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Operator summary | Auto-generated |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration narrative | Write-once |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | JSONL | Per-iteration structured delta | Write-once |
| `research.md` | Markdown | Final/progressive synthesis | Workflow-owned |
| `resource-map.md` | Markdown | Optional evidence-derived resource map | Workflow-owned |

The artifact directory is resolved by `resolveArtifactRoot(specFolder, 'research')` from `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/shared/review-research-paths.cjs`.

---

## 2. PACKET LOCATION

Root specs use:

```text
{spec_folder}/research/
```

Child-phase and sub-phase targets use flat-first behavior:

- first run with an empty local `research/` directory writes flat at `{spec_folder}/research/`;
- a `{basename(spec_folder)}-pt-NN` subfolder is allocated only when prior content already exists for a non-matching target;
- continuation runs reuse the existing matching packet.

This avoids an unnecessary `pt-01` wrapper on first runs.

**Example (first run on a child phase):** `.../026-graph.../019-system-hardening/001-initial-research/004-desc-regen/` writes to `004-desc-regen/research/` directly.

**Example (subsequent run with prior content for a different target):** `004-desc-regen/research/004-desc-regen-pt-02/` (pt-NN allocated as a sibling to the prior content).

### Frontier Node Store (expansive depth only)

`research/frontier-nodes.jsonl` is the node-status store for a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive`. A default run never creates it. Rows are append-only and carry `{nodeId, parentId, depth, scope, question, status, reason, iteration, dedupeKey}`. The last row for a node wins, so a status change is one more row rather than an edit. `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` is the only writer.

Statuses are `open`, `active`, `answered`, `blocked`, `exhausted`, `abstained` and `pruned`. A frontier question resolves when its node reaches a terminal status.

The reducer projects the store as `registry.frontier {nodes, stats, budget}`, applies the depth budget so an unspent share rolls down to the next depth only, closes a spent band's open nodes as `exhausted` with reason `budget`, and adds a `## 6B. FRONTIER` dashboard section.

---

## 3. OWNERSHIP MODEL

| Owner | Writes |
|-------|--------|
| Agent iteration | `iterations/iteration-NNN.md`, JSONL iteration/event append, optional progressive synthesis contribution |
| Workflow reducer | strategy machine-owned sections, findings registry, dashboard |
| Workflow synthesis | `research.md`, lifecycle snapshots |
| Spec anchoring protocol | bounded `spec.md` seed/context/fenced findings block |

The reducer is the source of truth for derived state. Manual edits to reducer-owned outputs are overwritten on the next refresh.

---

## 4. FILE PROTECTION

| Protection | Meaning |
|------------|---------|
| `immutable` | Created once and not modified after init |
| `append-only` | New JSONL records may be appended; existing records are not rewritten |
| `write-once` | Each iteration artifact is created once |
| `mutable` | Workflow may update the file under defined ownership rules |
| `auto-generated` | Reducer/workflow regenerates the full file |

The config file carries the protection map; details live in `state-reducer-registry.md`.

---

## 5. ROUTED REFERENCES

| Resource | Use When |
|----------|----------|
| `state-jsonl.md` | Need the JSONL config, iteration, event, lineage, graph, or blocked-stop schemas |
| `state-outputs.md` | Need markdown output structure for strategy, iterations, dashboard, research synthesis, or resource map |
| `state-reducer-registry.md` | Need reducer ownership, registry schema, validation, fault tolerance, or reconstruction |
| `../convergence/convergence.md` | Need STOP contract and legal-stop semantics |
| `../protocol/spec-check-protocol.md` | Need bounded `spec.md` anchoring and generated-fence write-back |

---

## 6. NON-GOALS

- Do not document `deep-review` state here; route to the sibling skill.
- Do not treat legacy aliases as write targets. The workflow reads legacy aliases only for migration windows and writes canonical `deep-research-*` names.
- Do not manually edit reducer-owned dashboard or registry files.

---

## 7. LEDGER VOCABULARY AND PROJECTION CEILING

### Registered vocabulary

The canonical dotted stems for this mode live in `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/deep-research-ledger-types.ts`:

- `DeepResearchEventStems` — the registered stems the ledger accepts.
- `DEEP_RESEARCH_STEM_PRODUCERS` — one entry per stem recording whether a mechanical producer emits it today (`spoken`, with the producer files) or why nothing does (`reserved`).

`.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/check-ledger-stem-producers.cjs` scans the workflow assets and runtime scripts and fails (exit 2) when a registered stem has no producer, a spoken stem has no emitter, or a reserved stem is emitted anyway.

### Which file is authoritative

`deep-research-state.jsonl` is authoritative while the mode's authority record (`authority-deep-research.json`, state `legacy_authoritative`) names the legacy writer. Once the record moves to `new_authoritative_reversible` or `new_authoritative_final` with `selectedWriter: "dark"`, the ledger is authoritative and the append gateway refreshes this file as a projection after each append.

### What a projection refresh can reproduce

The projection folds only registered stems, and its `run_initialized` arm rebuilds a thin config row: `type`, `topic`, `maxIterations`, `generation`, `timestamp`. A pre-flip config row written by the workflow carries more — `convergenceThreshold`, `minIterations`, `minIdeaObservations`, `antiConvergence`, `sessionId`, `executor`, `specFolder`, `createdAt` — and a refresh cannot rebuild those keys.

Because losing them silently would look like a complete projection, a replace that would drop keys from an existing `type: "config"` first row is refused with `ATTRIBUTION_COLLAPSE` (invariant `no-attribution-loss-on-replace`) before any bytes are written. An operator who hits it either migrates the attribution into a registered `deep_research.run_initialized` payload or moves the legacy file aside to accept the loss deliberately.

## 5. NEGATIVE KNOWLEDGE

Use `ruledOut` to preserve eliminated approaches.

```json
{
  "type": "iteration",
  "iteration": 3,
  "status": "complete",
  "ruledOut": [
    {
      "approach": "HTTP/3 multiplexing",
      "reason": "No server-side support in target environment",
      "evidence": "file:docs/protocols.md:42"
    }
  ]
}
```

Ruled-out directions should also appear in strategy and synthesis outputs.

---

## 6. GRAPH EVENTS

`graphEvents` records coverage graph mutations from an iteration.

```json
{
  "type": "iteration",
  "iteration": 4,
  "graphEvents": [
    {"type": "question", "id": "q-reconnect", "label": "How should reconnect work?"},
    {"type": "finding", "id": "f-backoff", "label": "Exponential backoff is required"},
    {"type": "edge", "from": "f-backoff", "to": "q-reconnect", "relation": "ANSWERS"}
  ]
}
```

Valid node types are `question`, `finding`, `claim`, and `source`; `edge` records connect nodes. IDs must be unique within a single `(specFolder, loopType, sessionId)` namespace. Reusing the same logical ID across independent sessions is allowed because the coverage graph uses a composite key.

---

## 7. EVENT RECORDS

Lifecycle event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "resumed",
  "mode": "research",
  "sessionId": "dr-2026-05-24T00-00-00Z",
  "parentSessionId": "dr-2026-05-24T00-00-00Z",
  "lineageMode": "resume",
  "continuedFromRun": 4,
  "generation": 1,
  "archivedPath": null,
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Blocked stop event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "blocked_stop",
  "mode": "research",
  "run": 7,
  "stopReason": "blockedStop",
  "blockedBy": ["keyQuestionCoverage", "sourceDiversity"],
  "recoveryStrategy": "focus_pivot",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Pause event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "userPaused",
  "mode": "research",
  "run": 7,
  "stopReason": "userPaused",
  "sentinelPath": "research/.deep-research-pause",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Stuck recovery event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "stuckRecovery",
  "mode": "research",
  "run": 7,
  "stopReason": "stuckRecovery",
  "fromFocus": "Connection pooling",
  "toFocus": "Transport alternatives",
  "strategy": "try_opposites",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Idea observation event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "idea_observed",
  "mode": "research",
  "run": 7,
  "ideaId": "idea-cache-stampede",
  "idea": "Investigate cache stampede mitigation as a follow-up focus",
  "category": "ideas",
  "source": "iteration-007.md",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Leaf agents may record `idea_observed` through the gateway for promising tangents. They must not record `idea_promoted`; promotion is reducer-owned.

Idea promotion event:

```json
{
  "type": "event",
  "event": "idea_promoted",
  "mode": "research",
  "run": 8,
  "ideaId": "idea-cache-stampede",
  "idea": "Investigate cache stampede mitigation as a follow-up focus",
  "category": "ideas",
  "observationCount": 2,
  "minIdeaObservations": 2,
  "firstObservedRun": 6,
  "lastObservedRun": 7,
  "timestamp": "2026-05-24T00:02:00Z"

# v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research

Deep research stops once its starting questions are answered. This release adds an opt-in mode that lets a run grow its own questions.

- Leaves propose new sub-questions while the run works.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the question set.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A run that answers its starting questions narrowly looks finished. Nothing could add a question once the run began. So a broad topic could stop early.

- Narrow coverage looked the same as converged coverage.
- The new mode lets the run widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a run with one flag](#c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Open questions exist before the first pass](#c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass) | The run has open sub-questions before iteration one. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Rejected proposals name their reason](#c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason) | A pruned proposal records the check that stopped it. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Focus goes to the shallowest open node](#c-focus-goes-to-the-shallowest-open-node) | The next iteration works the least deep open question. | Steering and Stopping |
| [A run keeps going while nodes stay open](#c-a-run-keeps-going-while-nodes-stay-open) | A run that looks finished continues while a node is open. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Budget is shared across three depth bands](#c-budget-is-shared-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits across depths. Unused budget rolls down. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Finished nodes stop counting as open](#c-finished-nodes-stop-counting-as-open) | A finished node resolves its question and records why it ended. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Lineages share the starting nodes](#c-lineages-share-the-starting-nodes) | A fan-out run deals its starting nodes across its lineages. | Fan-Out Runs |
| [The merge names a missing node](#c-the-merge-names-a-missing-node) | The merge warns when a starting node reached no lineage. | Fan-Out Runs |

&nbsp;

## Turning It On

Turning the mode on takes one flag. Without the flag, a run keeps its old behavior. Its dashboard gains no new section.

#### Widen a run with one flag <a id="c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag"></a>

- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a research run.
- Leave the flag out to keep the old behavior.
- Passing `--depth-mode=default` also keeps the old behavior.
- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md` finds the flag row.

#### Default runs keep their output <a id="c-default-runs-keep-their-output"></a>

- A default run writes the same registry, dashboard, focus and prompt pack as before.
- The stop config echo stays the same.
- The mode key appears only on expansive runs.
- CLI lineage prompts gain one paragraph in the agent persona.
- That paragraph has no effect on a default run.
- Its waiver awaits operator sign-off.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive run adds a `## 6B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size and pruned nodes.
- It also shows revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

&nbsp;

## Admitting New Questions

Leaves propose sub-questions as rows. The run checks each one before it changes the question set.

#### Open questions exist before the first pass <a id="c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass"></a>

- The run splits its topic into starting nodes before iteration one.
- The model running the loop writes those nodes.
- It works from the topic and its key questions.
- Every starting node passes the same checks.
- A repeated launch adds no second set of nodes.

#### Every proposal passes six fixed checks <a id="c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks"></a>

- The checks run in one fixed order.
- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
- Relevance needs evidence, a scope and a parent that exists.
- Dedupe refuses a question the run already holds.
- Depth stops at three levels.
- Fan-out caps each parent at six children.
- Fan-out also caps the total node count.
- A child cannot hold more budget than its parent.
- A cycle is refused.

#### Rejected proposals name their reason <a id="c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason"></a>

- The first failing check decides the reason.
- A pruned proposal keeps its reason in the node store.
- A pruned proposal never reaches the research inbox.
- An admitted proposal adds one question with origin `frontier`.

&nbsp;

## Steering and Stopping

Once nodes exist, the run works the shallowest one. It stops only when no node stays open within budget.

importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.15.17.0
---

# v1.15.17.0, Focus and Findings Checks Read the Leaf Fields

Two checks could not see the fields a leaf wrote. Both now read them. They run on every research run, with no flag.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- The state log keeps ten leaf fields.
- The list includes `findingsCount`, `keyQuestions` and `sourcesQueried`.
- The `findings_not_enumerated` gate reads the real `findingsCount`.
- `focusAlignment` is live on default research runs.
- It judges the latest record that carries both question lists.
- Reducer consumers now receive the fields.
- A single `keyQuestions` entry resolves its question.
- `sourcesQueried` feeds the source list.
- The dashboard shows `focusTrack` and `findingsCount`.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

The replay ran five real generation-two deltas. `focusAlignment` failed on four of them. The findings gate and route proof passed on all five. Iterations 2, 3 and 5 answered an unlisted question. Iteration 4 wrote its key question as a bare id, `Q6`. That id does not match the answered text.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

A `focusAlignment` failure can now hold a run at CONTINUE. The hold clears on the next aligned iteration. The guard judges only the latest record with both lists. The prompt pack asks for the exact question text. Write `keyQuestions` with no id prefix, such as `Q1:`.

&nbsp;

# v1.15.16.0, The Prompt Pack Names research.md and the Next-Focus Heading

The research prompt pack now agrees with the research agent definition on two points. A fan-out lineage can write its own synthesis. The reducer reads the next focus the pack asks for.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **The pack allows `research/research.md`.** ALLOWED WRITE PATHS lists `research.md` beside the iterations folder. It does so only when `progressiveSynthesis` is `true`.
- **The narrative heading is `Recommended Next Focus`.** The heading list ends in the heading the research reducer parses. The next focus is read from the pack's own wording.
- **A lockstep test guards both points.** It fails when the pack and the agent definition drift apart on either one.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. The agent definition and the runtime mirrors are unchanged.

title: "Divergent pivot seat cap and council retry"
description: "A divergent pivot council runs at most three seats per attempt, and a retryable first-attempt quorum failure reopens the pivot once as attempt 2."
trigger_phrases:

  - "council quorum retry"
  - "maxCouncilSeatOutputs"
  - "pivot attempt 2"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Divergent pivot seat cap and council retry

## 1. OVERVIEW

A divergent pivot runs a council of seats. The seat cap is three per council attempt. When the first attempt misses council quorum in a retryable way, the workflow reopens the pivot once as attempt 2, and that retry is the only re-entry.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seat Cap

`maxCouncilSeatOutputs` defaults to 3 in `runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts`. The research and review pivot scripts scope the seat count to the current pivot id and attempt.

### One Retry

When finalize reports a retryable council quorum failure, the research and review command workflows reopen the pivot once as attempt 2. The skip condition stops any further retry, so attempt 2 never reopens.

### Attempt Identity

Seat event ids include the attempt number, so attempt 2 does not reuse attempt 1 event ids or seat paths. `runtime/scripts/divergent-pivot.cjs` carries the attempt number and the retry signal through its `prepare` and `finalize` subcommands, which is the seam the workflows call.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` | Shared | Seat cap default, seat scope and retryable quorum result. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/divergent-pivot.cjs` | Script | Carries the attempt number and retry signal through `prepare` and `finalize`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/divergent-research-pivot.ts` | Script | Scopes the research seat count to the pivot id and attempt. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts` | Script | Scopes the review seat count to the pivot id and attempt. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Reopens the pivot once as attempt 2 after a retryable quorum failure. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/divergent-pivot.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Seat scope, the attempt-2 retry, and attempt identity cases. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/divergent-pivot-seat-scope.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Seat-count scope per pivot id and attempt. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/council/multi-seat-dispatch.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Multi-seat dispatch with any seat ids, unchanged by this behavior. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/manual-testing-playbook/council/divergent-council-quorum-retry.md` | Manual playbook | DLR-062: three seats per attempt and one retry after a first-attempt quorum miss. |

---

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md:115:Each eligible pivot runs one native, in-process Council round with exactly three distinct seats: analytical, critical, and pragmatic. If that round misses quorum, because a seat fails to return or returns a report that does not parse, the pivot reopens once as attempt 2 with three fresh seat outputs. A second quorum miss, or three valid seats that do not agree, fails the pivot closed. `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` caps one council attempt at 3 seat outputs, so a pivot records at most 6. It does not use an external CLI. The transaction requires all three seat returns to be parse-valid and at least two seats to materially endorse the same candidate without a high-severity blocker. Candidate sources are persisted unswept dimensions, search debt, producer-consumer gaps, negative-test gaps, and traceability gaps. Candidate generation rejects directions without in-scope evidence and directions that imply implementation, a fix, mutation, writing, or target expansion.

The YAML's per-iteration executor dispatch steps are satisfied by the lineage. Spawning a second CLI process for the same iteration is a route violation, not an optimization, and on codex it also fails outright (see `cli-external-orchestration/cli-codex/SKILL.md` Rule 18).

###### .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/focus-alignment-and-findings-gate-read-leaf-fields.md
---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that deep-research runs the findings gate and the focusAlignment guard on the fields each leaf writes, on every default research run. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run verify-iteration.vitest`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run convergence-script.vitest`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-projections-contract.vitest`
4. `rg -n "focusAlignment|normalizeQuestionForAlignment" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`
5. `rg -n "findingsCount|keyQuestions" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl`

### Expected

Commands 1 to 3 exit 0 with every case passing. The verify suite includes the findings-gate case on a `findingsCount` that arrives through the gateway and the route-proof case on the projected state row. The convergence suite includes the `focusAlignment` cases for a failing latest record, a recovery after a later aligned iteration and a `Q1: ` prefixed match. The projection suite asserts the ten leaf keys on a projected research iteration row. Command 4 prints the guard and its prefix normalizer. Command 5 prints the record shape with `findingsCount` and `keyQuestions`, and the rule that `keyQuestions` is the exact question text with no id prefix.

### Evidence

- The vitest summary line and exit status for commands 1 to 3.
- The `rg` line numbers for commands 4 and 5, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 to 3 exit 0, and commands 4 and 5 show the guard reading the latest record that carries both lists and the record shape naming both leaf fields.
- **Fail**: A check reads a field the projected row does not carry, `focusAlignment` holds a run after a later aligned iteration, or any named suite fails.

### Failure Triage

- The findings gate passes with nothing enumerated: check that the gate reads the projected `findingsCount`, not only the delta.
- `focusAlignment` holds a run after an aligned iteration: check that the guard reads the latest record that carries both lists.
- The projection lacks a leaf key: check the conditional spreads in the research contract projection and the ten-key assertion in the projection suite.

###### .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/next-focus-ignores-answered-free-text.md
---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that deep-research picks its next focus from open machine questions and the latest leaf's Recommended Next Focus, and not from a status sentence or an answered question. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "status sentence"`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "Recommended Next Focus"`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run continuity-thread.vitest`
4. `rg -n "buildCarriedForwardOpenQuestions|deriveNextFocusFromContinuity" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/continuity-thread.cjs`
5. `rg -n "resolveNextFocus|buildRegistry" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`

### Expected

Commands 1 to 3 exit 0 with every selected case passing. The case that drops a status sentence listed under Questions Remaining passes. The case that reads the latest leaf Recommended Next Focus, and skips it when it is resolved, passes. Commands 4 and 5 print the carried-forward filter, the leaf read and the call sites that feed the registry.

### Evidence

- The vitest summary line and exit status for commands 1 to 3, with the selected case names.
- The `rg` line numbers for commands 4 and 5, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 to 3 exit 0 with the named cases passing, and commands 4 and 5 show the filter and the leaf read.
- **Fail**: A status sentence or an answered question reaches Next Focus or the carried-forward list, or any named case fails.

### Failure Triage

- A named case is missing or renamed: the test file no longer pins the behavior, so restore the case before trusting the verdict.
- A status sentence reaches the carried-forward list: check the question-shape and answered-question filter in `buildCarriedForwardOpenQuestions`.
- Next Focus ignores an unresolved leaf recommendation while a machine question is open: check the leaf read in `deriveNextFocusFromContinuity`.

###### .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-research-run.md
---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off, so the converged run cannot re-stop at once, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--extend' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `rg -n 'run_extended' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run append-mode-event-legacy-seam.vitest workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest`
4. `/deep:research "<fixture topic>" :auto --spec-folder=<completed-fixture-spec-folder> --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off`
5. `rg -c '"event":"extended"' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-state.jsonl` (the `run_extended` stem lives in the ledger, and the state log carries its projected `extended` row)
6. `jq '{maxIterations, sessionId: .lineage.sessionId, generation: .lineage.generation, lineageMode: .lineage.lineageMode, continuedFromRun: .lineage.continuedFromRun}' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-config.json` (research keeps the lineage fields under `.lineage`)
7. `find <completed-fixture-spec-folder> -name 'synthesis-v*-r*.md'`
8. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest -t 'extend-minutes'` (the `--extend-minutes=M` cases)

### Expected

Command 1 prints the `--extend` binding and the extension step. Command 2 prints the `run_extended` stem and its payload type. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. After command 4 finishes, command 5 prints `1`, command 6 shows the original `sessionId`, generation 1, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3 and `lineageMode` `completed-continue`, and command 7 lists one snapshot file. The run adds exactly three iteration files. Command 8 exits 0 with 4 tests passed and none failed. They cover the `--extend-minutes` binding in both commands, the refusal of a value that is not a positive integer, and the case where `--extend-minutes` replaces the default added minutes.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, the command 3 summary line, the command 5 count, the command 6 JSON, the command 7 listing and the command 8 summary line, together with the iteration file count before and after the run.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: One `run_extended` event, the original `sessionId` and generation, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3, a `completed-continue` lineage, one synthesis snapshot, no `resumed` row, and exactly three new iterations.
- **Fail**: Any second `run_extended` event, a changed `sessionId` or generation, a different iteration cap, a missing snapshot, an overwritten snapshot, or a dispatch count other than three.

### Failure Triage

Check the state log first, then the config. A missing event with a correct config means the gateway append was skipped, so read the extension step in `deep-research.yaml` before the classifier override. A correct event with a wrong dispatch count points at the classifier, which should land on resume.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Refusal: run the same command with `--extend=0` and confirm it stops with a named usage error before any state write. The flag accepts positive integers only.
- Refusal: run it against a run that is still in progress and confirm it refuses because the run is not complete.
- Duration: on a fresh copy of the completed fixture, run command 4 with `--extend=3 --extend-minutes=30`. Then run `jq '{maxDurationMinutes, createdAt}' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-config.json`. `maxDurationMinutes` equals the larger of the prior budget and the minutes elapsed since `createdAt`, plus 30. `createdAt` keeps its original value. Without `--extend-minutes`, the added minutes default to the prior budget.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Start a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance and confirm that performance is accepted as a custom dimension, ordered after the four canonical dimensions, and listed in the review prompt.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "custom dimension" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
2. `rg -n "function buildDimensionUniverse" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-review-state-reducer.vitest -t "custom dimension"`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the review_dimensions input line, the risk-order line and the normalize rule among its other custom-dimension lines, each placing custom dimensions after the canonical four. Command 2 prints one line, the `buildDimensionUniverse(config)` definition. Command 3 exits 0 with the cases `tracks and covers a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` and `attributes a finding that names a custom dimension to it, not to the first addressed one` reported as passed, and no failed test. Command 4 exits 0 with `renders a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` reported as passed.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 matches for the normalize rule, the command 2 definition line, and the names and pass lines that command 3 and command 4 print.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The setup rule accepts a custom name and normalizes it, the universe builder places it after the four canonical names, and command 3 and command 4 exit 0 with the named cases passing.
- **Fail**: The setup rule rejects or drops the custom name, the universe places it before a canonical name, the prompt line omits it, or a named case fails.

### Failure Triage

If command 1 shows no custom-name wording, the workflow input or normalize rule has drifted, so check the review_dimensions line and the normalize rule in `deep-review.yaml`. If command 3 fails on the universe cases, read `buildDimensionUniverse` and `deriveDimension` in `reduce-state.cjs`, since the canonical names must come first and the configured names follow in order. If command 4 fails, read the `review_dimensions_list` binding in `deep-review.yaml` and the dimension line in `prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl`.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

function seedNodes({ artifactDir, iteration = 0, candidates, knownTexts = [], target = 'research' }) {
  // Every seeded node sits at depth 1 against the run's synthetic root: the seed
  // sees only the topic, so it cannot justify a deeper placement on its own.
  // A run is seeded once. A re-run of the seed step (a restart that kept the
  // store, a repeated fan-out launch) would otherwise add a reworded second
  // decomposition beside the first, and a fan-out partition would then name
  // nodes no lineage holds.
  const mode = assertTarget(target);
  const storePath = path.join(artifactDir, NODE_STORE_FILE);
  const existing = readNodeStore(storePath);
  if ([...existing.values()].some((node) => node.depth === 1)) {
    return { admitted: [], pruned: [], skipped: 'already-seeded' };

<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

> This document decides whether the packet may close. A packet is closeable when
> every row below is `Met`, `Waived` or `Superseded`. A `Waived` or `Superseded`
> row MUST name an ADR that exists in `decision-record.md`.

### Status values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `Met` | Verified. The Verification cell names evidence that was actually observed. |
| `Unmet` | Not yet satisfied. Blocks closure. |
| `Waived` | Deliberately not pursued. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |
| `Superseded` | Replaced by a different criterion or decision. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |

### Waiver cell

Write `-` when the row is `Met` or `Unmet`. Write `ADR-NNN` when the row is
`Waived` or `Superseded`, naming a decision record that exists in
`decision-record.md`. A waiver naming an ADR that is not there fails validation:
the point of a waiver is that someone recorded the reasoning, so an unbacked
waiver is treated as an unmet criterion rather than as a pass.

<!-- ANCHOR:what-built -->
## What Was Built

### Completed-Run Extension (--extend=N)

`/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` now reopen a completed run in the same session and generation. Under the loop lock and before classification, a new step counts the runs so far, sets `maxIterations` to that count plus N, and sets `maxDurationMinutes` to `max(prior budget, minutes since createdAt) + M`. It appends a `run_extended` ledger event through the gateway, and only then writes the config back with status `initialized` and lineage mode `completed-continue`. Classification then lands on resume, writes no `resumed` row, and the run continues. Before the first new dispatch the prior synthesis or review report is copied to `synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` or `review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`, and an existing snapshot is never overwritten.

`--extend` refuses a run that is not complete, refuses fan-out before any lineage spawns, and accepts positive integers only, as does `--extend-minutes`. Without `--extend`, nothing changes.

### Files Changed

| File | Action | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `runtime/lib/deep-{research,review}-ledger-schema/*-schema.ts`, `*-types.ts` | Modified | `run_extended` stem per loop, the `extension-budget` field kind, payload types, producer rows (`spoken`) |
| `runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/legacy-compatibility.ts` | Modified | Upcasts a legacy `extended` row and refuses one without a full budget |
| `runtime/lib/deep-{research,review}-reducer/*.ts` | Modified | Typed folds raise `maxIterations` and keep the run live |

58:### Completed-Run Extension (--extend=N)
60:`/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` now reopen a completed run in the same session and generation. Under the loop lock and before classification, a new step counts the runs so far, sets `maxIterations` to that count plus N, and sets `maxDurationMinutes` to `max(prior budget, minutes since createdAt) + M`. It appends a `run_extended` ledger event through the gateway, and only then writes the config back with status `initialized` and lineage mode `completed-continue`. Classification then lands on resume, writes no `resumed` row, and the run continues. Before the first new dispatch the prior synthesis or review report is copied to `synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` or `review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`, and an existing snapshot is never overwritten.
62:`--extend` refuses a run that is not complete, refuses fan-out before any lineage spawns, and accepts positive integers only, as does `--extend-minutes`. Without `--extend`, nothing changes.

### Lifecycle Branch Contract (current release)

The runtime supports four lineage modes. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation.
`fork` is deferred: it has no workflow wiring, so it MUST NOT be exposed to operators. If the long-form lineage feature is picked up later it will arrive with first-class event emission, reducer ancestry handling, and replay fixtures. Until then treat the contract below as canonical.

| Mode | Session id | Generation | Archive | JSONL event | When to pick |
|------|-----------|-----------|---------|-------------|--------------|
| `new` | fresh | 1 | n/a | implicit (config record) | no existing state |
| `resume` | same | same | none | `resumed` (see example above) | operator wants to continue the current lineage boundary |
| `restart` | fresh | `prior + 1` | prior `research/` tree moved under `research_archive/{timestamp}/` | `restarted` (same field set plus `archivedPath`) | operator wants to clear the workspace and replay with a new angle |
| `completed-continue` | same | same | none; the prior synthesis is copied to `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` | `extended` (see below) | a completed run needs N more iterations: pass `--extend=N` |

#### Completed-run extension

`--extend=N` (and optionally `--extend-minutes=M`) continues a run whose config has `status: "complete"`. Under the loop lock and before session classification, `step_extend_completed_run`:

1. Counts `continuedFromRun` as the distinct runs among the iteration records, error and timeout rows included.
2. Sets `maxIterations` to `continuedFromRun + N`, so a run stopped at 7 of 10 and extended by 3 ends at 10.
3. Sets `maxDurationMinutes` to `max(prior budget, minutes elapsed since createdAt) + M`. `M` defaults to the prior budget, and a run with no duration budget keeps none unless `--extend-minutes` is passed. `createdAt` is never re-stamped, because the duration check counts wall-clock time, idle time included.
4. Appends `deep_research.run_extended` through the append gateway, which projects an `extended` row into the state log, and only then replaces the config with `status: "initialized"`, `lineageMode: "completed-continue"` and `continuedFromRun`. A failed append leaves the run complete and untouched.

Classification then lands on resume. The workflow writes no `resumed` row for an extension, and the reducers treat an `extended` row like `resumed`, so the run is live again. Before the first new dispatch, `step_snapshot_extended_synthesis` copies the prior synthesis to `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` and refuses to overwrite an existing snapshot, so a second extension of the same generation keeps its own copy. `--extend` refuses a run that is not complete (use `--lineage-mode=resume`), refuses fan-out before any lineage spawns, and accepts positive integers only.

The extended row carries `{"type":"event","event":"extended","sessionId","generation","lineageMode":"completed-continue","continuedFromRun","extension","timestamp"}`, where `extension` holds `addedIterations`, `priorMaxIterations`, `newMaxIterations`, `priorMaxDurationMinutes`, `newMaxDurationMinutes` and `elapsedMinutesAtExtension`. It has no `archivedPath` or `parentSessionId`, so the field rule below covers `resumed` and `restarted` only.

**Convergence window.** Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new to cover re-stops on `all_questions_answered` at the first convergence check. The extension step prints a warning when this is likely; to keep the run going, pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`, or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`.

=== review 605-635
   ```
6. **Continue**: Enter the iteration loop from step_read_state

### Lifecycle Branches (current release)

The runtime supports four lineage modes. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation.
`fork` is deferred: it has no workflow wiring, so it MUST NOT be exposed to operators. If the long-form lineage feature is picked up later it will arrive with first-class event emission, reducer ancestry handling, and replay fixtures. Until then treat the contract below as canonical.

| Mode | Session id | Generation | Archive | JSONL event | When to pick |
|------|-----------|-----------|---------|-------------|--------------|
| `new` | fresh | 1 | n/a | implicit (config record) | no existing state |
| `resume` | same | same | none | `resumed` (see example above) | operator wants to continue the current lineage boundary |
| `restart` | fresh | `prior + 1` | prior `review/` tree moved under `review-archive/{timestamp}/` | `restarted` (same field set plus non-null `archivedPath`) | operator wants to clear the workspace and replay with a new angle |
| `completed-continue` | same | same | none; the prior review report is copied to `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` | `extended` (see below) | a completed run needs N more iterations: pass `--extend=N` |

#### Completed-run extension

`--extend=N` (and optionally `--extend-minutes=M`) continues a run whose config has `status: "complete"`. Under the loop lock and before session classification, `step_extend_completed_run`:

1. Counts `continuedFromRun` as the distinct runs among the iteration records, error and timeout rows included.
2. Sets `maxIterations` to `continuedFromRun + N`, so a run stopped at 7 of 10 and extended by 3 ends at 10.
3. Sets `maxDurationMinutes` to `max(prior budget, minutes elapsed since createdAt) + M`. `M` defaults to the prior budget, and a run with no duration budget keeps none unless `--extend-minutes` is passed. `createdAt` is never re-stamped, because the duration check counts wall-clock time, idle time included.
4. Appends `deep_review.run_extended` through the append gateway, which projects an `extended` row into the state log, and only then replaces the config with `status: "initialized"`, `lineageMode: "completed-continue"` and `continuedFromRun`. A failed append leaves the run complete and untouched.

Classification then lands on resume. The workflow writes no `resumed` row for an extension, and the reducers treat an `extended` row like `resumed`, so the run is live again. Before the first new dispatch, `step_snapshot_extended_review_report` copies the prior review report to `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` and refuses to overwrite an existing snapshot, so a second extension of the same generation keeps its own copy. `--extend` refuses a run that is not complete (use `--lineage-mode=resume`), refuses fan-out before any lineage spawns, and accepts positive integers only.

The extended row carries `{"type":"event","event":"extended","sessionId","generation","lineageMode":"completed-continue","continuedFromRun","extension","timestamp"}`, where `extension` holds `addedIterations`, `priorMaxIterations`, `newMaxIterations`, `priorMaxDurationMinutes`, `newMaxDurationMinutes` and `elapsedMinutesAtExtension`. It has no `archivedPath` or `parentSessionId`, so the field rule below covers `resumed` and `restarted` only.

**Convergence window.** Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new to cover re-stops on `all_dimensions_clean` at the first convergence check. The extension step prints a warning when this is likely; to keep the run going, pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`.

**Consecutive failures.** A run that completed on consecutive failures re-stops at the consecutive-failure check even under `--convergence-mode=off`, because that check runs before the `off` branch and the trailing failed iterations are still in the log.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1,120p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs
#!/usr/bin/env node

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// MODULE: Frontier Admission
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// The frontier node store is append-only, one JSON object per line, and the
// LAST row per nodeId wins. Status has to survive a reduce and a resume, and
// neither the inbox (six fields) nor the registry (rebuilt from its inputs on
// every reduce) can carry it, so it lives here instead.
//
// This module is the store's only writer: seed, admit, prune and close all pass
// through it. The admission checks run in one fixed order so the workflow steps
// have a single deterministic implementation rather than prose the orchestrating
// model executes: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, monotone budget, acyclicity.
// The first failing check prunes the proposal with that check's name as the
// reason.
//
// Only an admitted node reaches the inbox, and the inbox is the only path that
// grows the counted question set mid-run. A pruned node is recorded in the store
// and nowhere else.
//
// A review run reads the same store with the review config instead: an admitted
// node is queued by the store alone, and relevance narrows to the review target,
// so no counted question is invented where the run answers none.
//
// Leaves propose; they never call this module.

'use strict';

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const NODE_STORE_FILE = 'frontier-nodes.jsonl';
const INBOX_FILE = 'inbox.jsonl';
const CONFIG_FILE = 'deep-research-config.json';
const REVIEW_CONFIG_FILE = 'deep-review-config.json';

// A target names the loop a call speaks for: which config file it reads and
// whether an admitted node queues a question.
const TARGETS = Object.freeze(['research', 'review']);

const DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH = 3;
const DEFAULT_MAX_CHILDREN_PER_NODE = 6;
const DEFAULT_MAX_FRONTIER_NODES = 10;

// The seed step decomposes a target into a documented number of depth-1 nodes:
// 3 to 6 sub-questions for research, 3 to 8 sub-targets for review. The root's
// children are those nodes, so its cap is the top of that range, never below the
// per-node cap. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so this cannot
// come from config alone.
const ROOT_CHILDREN_FLOOR = Object.freeze({ research: 6, review: 8 });

const NODE_STATUSES = Object.freeze([
  'open',
  'active',
  'answered',
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
  'pruned',
]);

// close cannot move a node back to the unopened or admitted state: seeding and
// admission own those, and a re-close is only meaningful for a lifecycle move.
const CLOSE_STATUSES = new Set([
  'active',
  'answered',
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
  'pruned',
]);

// A node closed with one of these never answered its question: the branch ran
// out, or it was dropped. The graph projection has to keep that visible.
const UNANSWERED_CLOSE_STATUSES = new Set([
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
]);

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. HELPERS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function isPlainObject(value) {
  return Boolean(value) && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function isNonEmptyString(value) {
  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '';
}

function hasScope(scope) {
  if (typeof scope === 'string') return scope.trim() !== '';
  return Array.isArray(scope) && scope.length > 0;
}

function normalizeIteration(value) {
  return Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0 ? value : 0;
}

function capValue(value, fallback) {
  return Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0 ? Math.floor(value) : fallback;
}

function assertTarget(target) {
  if (!TARGETS.includes(target)) {
    throw new Error(`unknown target: ${String(target)}; expected ${TARGETS.join(' or ')}`);
  }
  return target;

## What Was Built

A documentation-only pass over the expansive-depth program. Each playbook package was checked against the Feature to Playbook Map, the index gaps the check found were fixed, and the four system-deep-loop feature catalogs gained entries for the 001-013 behavior. No runtime, command, YAML or JSON file changed.

2:title: "deep-review v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review"
3:description: "Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. An opt-in expansive depth mode admits, steers toward and closes them. Runs without the flag keep their output."
7:  - "expansive depth mode"
14:# v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review
16:Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. This release adds an opt-in expansive depth mode for that.
44:| [Sub-targets exist before the first pass](#c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets) | The review splits its target before iteration one. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
49:| [Budget splits across three depth bands](#c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths. | Steering and Stopping |
62:- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a `/deep:review` run.
63:- Leave the flag out, or pass `--depth-mode=default`, to keep the old behavior.
64:- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md` finds the flag row.
77:- The section shows frontier size, pruned sub-targets, revisits and budget spent at each depth.
86:#### Sub-targets exist before the first pass <a id="c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets"></a>
88:- The review splits its target into depth-one sub-targets before iteration one.
96:- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
99:- Depth stops at three levels.
100:- Fan-out caps each parent at six children and caps the total count.
107:- A sub-target that arrives with a reason is settled at seed time.
108:- Two seed reasons exist: `merge-no-own-diff` for a merge with no diff of its own, and `generated-artifact` for a generated index rebuild.
129:- The iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, the off setting, the max-iterations policy and the review floor still decide first.
132:#### Budget splits across three depth bands <a id="c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands"></a>
134:- The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths one, two and three.
141:- It seeds one revisit direction for each sub-target the frontier closed as `exhausted` or `blocked`.
148:A fan-out review deals its depth-one sub-targets across its lineages. The merge then checks the split.
152:- Open depth-one sub-targets deal round-robin across the lineages, in node-id order.
168:The default path was checked against a baseline. The baseline was captured before any review depth-mode code existed. The suites below ran on this branch.
174:| Count of `--depth-mode` in the review command reference | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the flag row |
175:| Count of `"depthMode"` in the review config | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the `default` value |
187:<summary><b>Tests and scenarios</b></summary>
198:- Review playbook validator: PASS, with the seven new scenarios.
216:- Add `--depth-mode=expansive` to a review run to let it add sub-targets.

# Source quality class (quality_class)

## 1. OVERVIEW

The graph upsert sets `quality_class` on each research SOURCE node from where the source lives. A `quality_class` the leaf supplies is overwritten, so a leaf cannot raise source diversity by naming a class. The source-diversity signal reads the derived value.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Derivation

`runtime/scripts/upsert.cjs` derives the class from the source `path`, then `name`, then `id`, and writes it on research SOURCE nodes. The derived value replaces any leaf-supplied `quality_class`.

### Closed Vocabulary

`runtime/lib/coverage-graph/coverage-graph-query.ts` exports the frozen `SOURCE_QUALITY_CLASS` vocabulary: `repo-code`, `repo-test`, `repo-spec`, `skill-doc`, `ledger-state`, `external-doc`, `external-other` and `unclassified`.

### Diversity Signal

The source-diversity signal reads the derived class from each SOURCE node, not a value the leaf wrote.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/upsert.cjs` | Script | Derives and writes `quality_class` in the node loop for research SOURCE nodes. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/coverage-graph/coverage-graph-query.ts` | Shared | Exports the closed `SOURCE_QUALITY_CLASS` vocabulary and the safe metadata keys. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/upsert-script.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Derivation and strip cases. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-graph-upsert.vitest.ts` | Vitest | A leaf-supplied value does not survive the research upsert path. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/coverage-graph-signals.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Fixtures seeded through `upsert` whose source names derive distinct classes. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/manual-testing-playbook/coverage-graph/source-quality-class-derivation.md` | Manual playbook | DLR-061: derived `quality_class` on research SOURCE nodes. |

// A research SOURCE node's quality class comes from where the source lives, never
// from a value the leaf sends, so source diversity rises only when a finding cites
// sources of genuinely different kinds. `classes` is the frozen vocabulary the
// coverage-graph query module exports, so no class name is repeated here.
const DOC_HOST_PREFIXES = ['docs.', 'developer.', 'learn.', 'devdocs.'];
const DOC_HOSTS = new Set(['developer.mozilla.org', 'nodejs.org', 'typescriptlang.org', 'www.typescriptlang.org', 'vitest.dev']);
const DOC_EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.md', '.mdx', '.txt', '.rst']);
const TEST_FILE_PATTERN = /\.(?:test|spec|vitest)\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/i;
const TEST_DIR_NAMES = new Set(['test', 'tests', '__tests__']);

function classifySourceLocation(value, classes) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
  const text = value.trim();
  if (!text) return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
  if (/^https?:\/\//i.test(text)) {
    let host = '';
    try {
      host = new URL(text).hostname.toLowerCase();
    } catch {
      return classes.EXTERNAL_OTHER;
    }
    const isDocHost = DOC_HOSTS.has(host)
      || DOC_HOST_PREFIXES.some((prefix) => host.startsWith(prefix))
      || host.endsWith('.readthedocs.io');
    return isDocHost ? classes.EXTERNAL_DOC : classes.EXTERNAL_OTHER;
  }
  if (/\s/.test(text)) return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
  const repoPath = text.replace(/^\.\//, '').replace(/:\d+(?:-\d+)?$/, '');
  const segments = repoPath.split('/').filter(Boolean);
  const fileName = segments[segments.length - 1] || '';
  const extension = path.extname(fileName).toLowerCase();
  if (segments.length < 2 && !extension) return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
  if (TEST_FILE_PATTERN.test(fileName) || segments.some((segment) => TEST_DIR_NAMES.has(segment))) return classes.REPO_TEST;
  if (extension === '.jsonl' || /ledger/i.test(fileName)) return classes.LEDGER_STATE;
  if (segments[0] === 'specs') return classes.REPO_SPEC;
  if (DOC_EXTENSIONS.has(extension)) return classes.SKILL_DOC;
  return classes.REPO_CODE;
}

// path outranks the label, and the label outranks the id: the first field that
// names a real location decides the class.
function deriveSourceQualityClass(node, classes) {
  for (const candidate of [node.path, node.name, node.label, node.id]) {
    const qualityClass = classifySourceLocation(candidate, classes);
    if (qualityClass !== classes.UNCLASSIFIED) return qualityClass;
  }
  return classes.UNCLASSIFIED;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
